#6931. IDC Report Finds Significant Gap Between AI Spending and Measurable Financial Returns

Nearly 70% of organizations report a significant gap between artificial intelligence spending and measurable financial returns, according to a recent survey by IDC.

The research firm's 2026 Worldwide AI Economics and Cost of Ownership Survey found that 69% reported such a gap, while only 15% had achieved an AI return on investment exceeding 100%.

In a separate May 2026 survey, 73% of respondents identified excessive AI costs as a significant risk.

IDC highlighted the findings in an October 20 analysis examining what enterprise customers expect from AI technology vendors.

Enterprise customers are placing greater emphasis on measurable business outcomes when evaluating AI products, according to IDC.

The firm identified three priorities for vendors: demonstrating financial value, addressing governance and security requirements, and building support beyond IT departments.

The analysis also highlighted the gap between initial agentic AI deployments and broader adoption. Although 80% of organizations have started deploying agentic AI, only 17% are using it across the enterprise.

IDC said evaluating AI investments can be difficult when the technology changes how employees work and how business processes operate.

The firm expects AI infrastructure capacity to catch up with demand around 2029, potentially easing costs. Until then, vendors face pressure to demonstrate the long-term financial value of their products.

AI funding decisions involve a broad range of executives. IDC's September 2026 C-Suite Tech Survey found that CIOs and CTOs were involved in 63% of those decisions, business-function executives in 62%, and chief financial officers in 61%.

A separate IDC survey found that 42% of CEOs planned to hire a chief AI officer within the next 12 months.

Buyers also reported concerns about the products and terms vendors offer. Limited integration with existing systems, insufficient model and data transparency, and concerns about vendor lock-in were each cited by 34% of respondents.

Overpromising and underdelivering, along with unclear pricing models, were each cited by 31%.

IDC said vendors need to substantiate financial returns and address security and governance requirements while working with decision-makers across IT, finance, and other business departments.

For more information, go to the IDC blog.

The heads of OpenAI, Anthropic, and Hugging Face recently joined AI researcher Yoshua Bengio at the United Nations headquarters in New York to warn the UN Security Council about the security risks of increasingly capable AI.

Sign-in problems account for up to 53% of help desk tickets at individual enterprises, but network issues can take significantly longer to resolve, according to a recent study from ControlUp.

CrowdStrike's latest Threat Hunting Report traces a multiyear shift from conventional intrusions toward attacks that exploit trusted identities, cloud services, AI systems, and software dependencies.

Anthropic's new Claude release, Sonnet 5.5, brings cybersecurity safeguards that can change which model handles a request during a Claude conversation. Higher-risk tasks can fall back to Sonnet 5.

#6926. We Tested Dozens of Perfumes to Find the 5 Best Baccarat Rouge 540 Dupes—Here’s Our Ranking

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

"Are you wearing Baccarat Rouge?" This might be the greatest compliment—someone noticing that I've doused myself in a perfume that cost more than my very first cell phone. This expensive fragrance is a total status symbol and has gone viral more than a handful of times. The poetic blend of spicy saffron and effervescent jasmine mingle with warm amber and cedar to create a luxe, mysterious, soft perfume that automatically makes anyone more attractive, IMO. It's pretty iconic if you ask me. But the price, at $360 for a 2.4-oz bottle, is high enough to give someone a heart attack.

Now that the perfume is so popular, a wave of Baccarat Rouge 540 dupes has hit the market from brands like Dossier and Fine’ry. Plus, many brands have been inspired by the fresh and woodsy DNA of the original to create their own iterations that smell shockingly similar. Can they really hold a candle to the O.G.? I set out on a mission to sniff all of the so-called "dupes" on the market and rank them for how similar they smell to the original. I found some really good options.

Now, there’s no replicating something as iconic as BR540, but these five affordable picks come pretty darn close if I do say so myself.

We can't break down the dupes without acknowledging the original, okay? Baccarat Rouge 540, by Maison Francis Kurkdjian (one of the most famous French perfumers of the 21st century who owns his own brand but is also the creative director of perfumery at Christian Dior), was developed to celebrate the 250th anniversary of the Baccarat crystal line. The note breakdown is actually defined by three "auras," according to Kurkdjian: A sparkling opening, followed by a warm middle, and a woodsy and sweet base.

I've been using this perfume on and off for the last five years, and it's still one of the most unique scents I own. The way it smells upon first spritz versus how it develops on my skin is truly a nod to perfume artistry (sorry, I'm getting nerdy here). There's a reason everyone is so obsessed with this jasmine-dominant fragrance: It's light and airy yet warm and enveloping—it's really unlike any other perfume (you know, until everyone started making similar versions). Yes, it's extremely expensive, but all you need is one spritz to last all day long, meaning the bottle will last a pretty long time.

If you're a little scared of the price tag, we've got a slew of cheaper options below.

Although Dossier now creates many of its own original fragrances, the brand started with creating affordable dupes to expensive, iconic perfumes. So it's no surprise that Ambery Saffron, its counterpart to Baccarat Rouge 540, smells the most similar of every option I've sniffed. It's not exact when you place them side by side, but on skin, I can't really tell that it's two different perfumes.

I'd say Ambery Saffron is a bit more woodsy than Baccarat, which dries down extremely sweet on my skin. But that's likely because it doesn't have the ambroxan and sugary notes that give BR540 that syrupy quality. However, I really do love the addition of orange blossom in this Dossier perfume. It creates a teensy hint of citrus that works well with jasmine and saffron.

Everything Sol De Janeiro does is tropical in nature. So the Cheirosa '68 body and hair mist is basically what you’d get if you swirled Baccarat Rouge with some beachy notes. This fruity scent is much juicier than the original BR540, all thanks to pink dragonfruit. It's more mouthwatering and brighter than the sparkling scent of Baccarat Rouge, too, making it perfect to spray on alongside your Baccarat Rouge (or a dupe) or if you want something a bit more luminous. Consider it Baccarat’s carefree, summery cousin.

Surprisingly, this celebrity perfume bears a striking resemblance to Baccarat Rouge. While the notes appear slightly different, the sparkling quality and hints of sweetness are reminiscent of what you get with the jasmine-based juice. But what I loved most about this when I spritzed it all over is how long it lasts. Ariana is busy, so she can't be reapplying her scent all the time. I get it! And this fragrance proves it, with impressive longevity on skin and clothes. One spritz on the top of my hand, and I'll be sniffing the pear, praline, vanilla, and musk notes for days.

Fine’ry is known for creating similar versions of our favorite perfumes at super affordable prices. And, bonus, you can easily add to your Target orders. (Score!) The New Rouge—their take on Baccarat, of course—is a sexy, warm scent for everyone.

The sandalwood gives this a much deeper profile than the original. If Baccarat Rouge smells too traditionally "feminine" to you, then you'd probably love this. It's not nearly as sweet, leaning more into the warm, spiciness with an undertone of woodsiness. I would wear this more in the fall/winter or in the evening for that reason.

Sure, the bottles take me right back to high school. But this scent feels a lot more grown up than what I spritzed back then. In fact, this was the first-ever Baccarat Rouge 540 dupe I tried—and it's still one of the best to this day.

The notes are mostly different, but hit a lot of the same ideas. There's sandalwood for a grounding scent, amber for the cozy factor, and a subtle sweetness from sugar and musk. Unlike BR540, though, this one uses starflower (fitting, considering the name) and tangelo to create the sparkling quality of the original. It's definitely not the same scent, but you could probably fool some unsuspecting noses. And best of all, this has some serious lasting power and projection. You'd think you were wearing an expensive eau de parfum, not an under-$20 body spray.

Cosmo editors are constantly testing fragrances to share the best options on the market to you (as well as include in our beauty awards). To compile this list, I...

If you’re looking for a dupe of the iconic Baccarat Rouge 540, chances are you’re probably on a budget. So before you start your shopping journey, make sure you set a target price range and stick to it. All of the options on our list are under $100—and some are even under $30—so there’s a truly excellent alt for everyone.

Disclaimer: While all of our picks are worthy alternatives to the O.G. Baccarat Rouge 540, none of them smell exactly the same (even if they come v close). If you want to be as close to the original as possible, pay close attention to the key notes to make sure they match up. BR540 features a mix of saffron, jasmine, ambergris, and cedar, and plenty of the perfumes listed above also contain all or a mix of the same.

Mary Honkus is a contributor for Cosmopolitan with five years of experience researching, writing, and editing beauty stories, including reviewing every Maison Margiela Replica perfume and rounding up the best winter perfumes. She’s an authority in all beauty categories, but an expert when it comes to fragrance—her scent wardrobe is ever-expanding and she regularly rotates through a collection of over 600 perfumes (including a few of the options in this article). For this story, she spritzed and sniffed over 40 perfumes to find the ones that most resembled the iconic Maison Francis Kurkdjian Baccarat Rouge 540.

Beth Gillette is the beauty editor at Cosmopolitan and has seven years of experience researching, writing, and editing skincare stories that range from reviewing Glossier perfumes to Le Labo Santal 33 dupes. She regularly tests and analyzes perfumes while working with the industry’s top fragrance experts to assess new formulas, brands, and trends.

Beth Gillette is the beauty editor at Cosmopolitan, where she covers skincare, makeup, hair, nails, and more across digital and print. She can generally be found in bright eyeshadow furiously typing her latest feature or hemming and hawing about a new product you "have to try." Prior to Cosmopolitan, she wrote and edited beauty content as an Editor at The Everygirl for four years. Follow her on Instagram for makeup selfies and a new hair 'do every few months.

#6922. Report: Global Ransomware Attacks Rise 23% in Q3

Global ransomware activity increased 23% in the third quarter of 2026, with organizations in North America and Europe accounting for the largest share of attacks, according to cybersecurity firm NCC Group.

The company's Cyber Threat Intelligence Report Review of Q3 2026 recorded 2,865 ransomware victim claims, up from 2,325 in the previous quarter.

Despite the quarterly increase, attacks declined 26% month over month in September to 821. North America recorded the most attacks that month with 390, followed by Europe with 211.

The industrial sector accounted for 29% of quarterly attacks, while Qilin remained the most active ransomware group for the sixth consecutive quarter, recording 365 victims. The Gentlemen followed with 356, and INC Ransom ranked third with 109.

The report highlighted a July cyber attack against a small UK power generation facility that forced operations to stop for approximately four days.

Although the incident did not affect the national electricity grid, it demonstrated how cyber attacks can disrupt physical infrastructure.

The security researchers said the incident was consistent with techniques previously associated with Iranian-affiliated campaigns, including attacks against internet-exposed industrial controllers.

However, the UK government has not formally attributed the attack, and the techniques used remain unconfirmed.

The report also identified growing security challenges as organizations connect traditional IT networks with operational technology (OT) systems for remote maintenance and centralized management.

The firm recommended network segmentation, stronger privileged-access controls, and continuous monitoring of OT environments.

The research found that more than 40 vulnerabilities affecting enterprise applications, VPN appliances, e-mail gateways, and management platforms were added to CISA's Known Exploited Vulnerabilities Catalog during the quarter.

The report examined Gunra, a ransomware-as-a-service operation that has targeted 53 victims across multiple industries as of Q3.

According to the cybersecurity company, Gunra exploited vulnerabilities CVE-2024-55591 and CVE-2025-24472 in internet-facing Fortinet devices to gain access to corporate networks.

The group also used stolen credentials, session hijacking, and administrative tools to move between systems before deploying ransomware.

Other emerging ransomware groups included Deadlock and Storm, with the latter claiming 78 victims by the end of September.

Researchers also observed initial access brokers advertising compromised VPN, Remote Desktop Protocol, and SSH access, alongside access to cloud identities, source code repositories, and security management platforms.

The quarterly review also highlighted BraZetsu, an AI-enhanced Windows malware framework that evolved into a specialized initial access toolkit.

According to the report, BraZetsu uses AI models to automate victim profiling, analyze compromised environments, and identify valuable corporate systems.

The framework searches for enterprise software, financial data, digital certificates, and cloud credentials, helping operators identify compromised access that can be sold to other cyber criminals.

The cybersecurity firm also identified risks involving legitimate AI agents, including prompt injection, excessive permissions, and unintended access to external systems.

The report cited incidents involving U.S. and Australian government systems as examples of agents accessing resources beyond their intended scope.

Researchers warned that these incidents do not necessarily involve malicious intent but can create consequences resembling conventional cyber intrusions.

For the full report, visit the NCC Group site.

#6917. Microsoft Expands Protections and Controls for Sovereign Cloud

Microsoft is introducing new tools within its sovereign cloud offerings designed to give organizations tighter control over where their data resides, who can access it, and how critical systems operate, especially in light of AI becoming a bigger part of enterprise infrastructure.

In a recent announcement, Douglas Phillips, president and chief technology officer of Microsoft Specialized Clouds, outlined updates spanning public cloud, private cloud, and partner environments. The changes target governments, regulated industries, and organizations that need tighter control over sensitive workloads without abandoning cloud-based AI capabilities.

"Digital sovereignty should give organizations greater control and security over the technology they depend on, while continuing to innovate," Phillips wrote.

One of the biggest additions is Microsoft's Sovereign Control Panel, which will enter public preview at Microsoft Ignite. Available through the Azure portal, the tool will give IT administrators one place to manage data sovereignty settings, spot compliance issues, and identify potential risks that could disrupt operations.

Microsoft is also tightening controls over who can access sensitive systems. Its Data Guardian feature will require oversight by personnel based in Europe when Microsoft engineers access certain production systems in the European Union and European Free Trade Association. Another tool, Production Touch Ledger, will keep tamper-resistant records of that access, giving organizations a clearer picture of who accessed their systems and what they did.

Microsoft is also giving customers more control over encryption. External key management with Azure Managed HSM is now generally available, letting organizations keep their main encryption keys on hardware they own or manage outside Microsoft's cloud.

The company is also expanding its Sovereign Private Cloud offerings, giving organizations more options to run systems with or without an internet connection. New capabilities include GitHub Enterprise Local, GitHub Copilot with locally hosted AI models, and additional data services running on Azure Local.

The push for greater operational control comes as Microsoft is also reconsidering how enterprises should secure increasingly autonomous AI systems.

As reported by Redmond magazine, Microsoft is expanding Project Perception to address risks posed by what the company calls "agentic insiders," AI agents capable of accessing sensitive information and performing actions without constant human supervision.

That initiative introduces plans for Guardian Agents, specialized security agents designed to monitor other AI systems, evaluate their behavior and intervene when their actions become risky.

"Monitoring and securing those agents must become continuous," said Hayete Gallot, executive vice president of Microsoft Security.

Project Perception is about limiting what AI agents can do. Microsoft's sovereign cloud expansion tackles a different set of questions: where those systems run, who gets to administer them, and how the data stays protected.

Taken together, the two efforts point to a shift in Microsoft's enterprise AI strategy. The company is putting more weight on oversight, not just automation.

Phillips said AI is making these questions more pressing as organizations roll out intelligent systems across sensitive business processes and critical infrastructure.

For more information, go to the Microsoft blog.

Chris Paoli (@ChrisPaoli5) is the associate editor for Converge360.

New research suggests that the value of AI should be measured not only by the work it eliminates, but also by the instructional insight and human connection it preserves.

#6914. Your Vagina Has a Party Trick, and It's Called Pompoir

Meet the sex technique that puts your pelvic floor muscles to work.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

If you have a vagina (or happen to be particularly fond of them), there's a decent chance you've heard of Kegels. You know, those pelvic floor exercises that involve squeezing and releasing muscles you might not think about all that often, but the second someone mentions them, you immediately start clenching (like right now).

But what if you could put those same muscles to work during sex? That's where pompoir comes in, using different rhythms and contractions to create new sensations during penetration, sometimes without moving your hips at all.

Sometimes referred to as vaginal gymnastics, pompoir takes things a step further than your standard Kegel routine. “Think of Kegels as going to the gym and pompoir as playing the sport,” says Karen Toubi, DO, a board-certified OB/GYN and menopause and vulvovaginal disorders specialist at Lavida Women's Health, Beverly Hills.

But before you start treating your vagina like it's training for the Olympics, there's something worth knowing: Pompoir isn't about permanently tightening your vagina, performing for your partner, or guaranteeing better orgasms. It's another way to explore pleasure, provided you're doing it safely and actually enjoying yourself.

Here's how pompoir works, how to try it, and what to know before giving it a go.

Unlike regular Kegels, pompoir is less about individual squeezes and more about controlling the timing, rhythm, and intensity of those contractions during sex. The idea is to create different sensations around a partner's penis or sex toy without relying entirely on thrusting.

The muscles doing the work are the same ones you engage during Kegels, including a group called the levator ani, which helps support your pelvic organs. And while you might be able to squeeze those muscles on command, getting them to cooperate in different rhythms takes a little more finesse. “Control comes with practice, like any motor skill,” says Liz Miracle, a pelvic floor physical therapist and head of clinical quality and education at Origin.

As for what it feels like? The person practicing pompoir might notice an internal pulsing sensation, while their partner could feel anything from a gentle squeeze to a fluttering movement. Of course, everyone's experience is different, and some partners might barely notice the contractions at all.

Before trying pompoir during sex, it helps to get familiar with your pelvic floor muscles on your own. Here's where to start.

Lie on your back with your knees bent and imagine you're trying to stop yourself from peeing or passing gas. Feel that gentle squeeze and lift? That's your pelvic floor doing its thing. Just don't actually practice by stopping your pee midstream, since that can interfere with normal bladder emptying.

“Your glutes, thighs, and belly should stay quiet,” says Miracle. Focus on squeezing and fully releasing the muscles without holding your breath. If you're struggling to figure out whether you're doing it correctly, a pelvic floor physical therapist can help.

Once you've identified the right muscles, try changing up the rhythm. Dr. Toubi suggests holding a contraction for up to 10 seconds before fully relaxing, or trying quick squeezes followed by an immediate release. You can also play around with different patterns, like two slow contractions followed by a faster one.

But don't focus solely on how hard you can squeeze. Amanda Neri, PT, DPT, pelvic health expert at Smile Makers Collection, emphasizes that learning to fully relax your pelvic floor between contractions is just as important as strengthening it. Otherwise, you risk creating unnecessary tension.

According to Bryony Lees, sex and relationships educator at Little Leaf Agency, missionary, cowgirl, and spooning can all be good positions for practicing pompoir, especially if you can keep your hips relatively still. Choose whichever feels most comfortable, and have your partner stay relatively still while you experiment with different squeezes and releases.

And yes, tell your partner what you're doing. Ask what feels good, pay attention to your own sensations, and don't feel like you need to master some elaborate routine to make it worthwhile. If anything hurts, stop.

It can, depending on the person. For the person practicing it, pompoir may increase body awareness and make certain sensations more noticeable. Some people also report stronger orgasms, since the pelvic floor muscles are involved in climaxing.

“For some people, yes, it can improve sex. It isn’t a guaranteed upgrade, though, and lots of people have great sex without ever thinking about this,” says Miracle. While research suggests pelvic floor muscle training can improve sexual function, pompoir specifically hasn't been studied enough to say whether it delivers the same benefits.

And despite what you might have heard, tighter doesn't automatically mean better. In fact, an overly tense pelvic floor can make sex painful. As Dr. Toubi explains, pompoir is about developing control, not permanently tightening your vagina or proving how good you are in bed. Reminder: Sex isn't a performance review.

Generally, yes, but that doesn't mean you should spend every spare minute squeezing your pelvic floor muscles. Overdoing it can lead to muscle tension, pelvic pain, urinary urgency, and even painful sex.

“A healthy pelvic floor needs to relax as well as squeeze,” says Dr. Toubi. If your muscles are already overly tense, adding more contractions could actually make things worse.

If you experience pain during sex, have vaginismus, chronic pelvic pain, or symptoms of pelvic organ prolapse, check with a pelvic floor physical therapist before trying pompoir. The same goes for anyone experiencing urinary leakage or other pelvic floor symptoms. If you've recently given birth or had pelvic surgery, talk to your healthcare provider about when it's safe to start.

Ultimately, pompoir should be something you try because you're curious and it feels good, not because you think your vagina needs improving. There are plenty of other ways to have great sex; no vaginal workouts required.

#6913. Cisco Intros Privileged Infrastructure Access for People and AI Agents

Cisco has introduced Privileged Infrastructure Access, an approach to infrastructure administration that gives people and AI agents temporary, task-specific permissions instead of leaving standing privileges available after their work is done.

In a recent blog post, Cisco's Matt Caulfield described a system that uses cryptographic identity to connect an administrator or agent to the infrastructure needed for a particular task. Access is governed by short-lived privileges, with session recording providing a record of the work performed.

"If there is no work being done, no privilege exists," Caulfield wrote. Cisco positions the offering as a way to bring identity controls directly into infrastructure operations while reducing dependence on static credentials, shared secrets, and broad access.

The announcement follows Cisco's previously announced Teleport partnership and its Infrastructure Identity concept, which connects the identity of a person or machine with the resources it can access, the actions it performs, and the permissions governing those actions.

Caulfield described a gap between the controls organizations apply to workforce applications and those used to administer network infrastructure. Companies have invested in single sign-on, multifactor authentication, and risk-based controls for application access, he wrote, but those protections often do not follow an engineer logging into a router or switch.

Instead, infrastructure access may depend on long-lived credentials, shared accounts, or separate policy systems. According to Cisco, that separation makes it harder to enforce least privilege and establish which person or system performed a particular action.

The company illustrates the problem with an administrator updating hundreds of switches during a maintenance window. If some updates fail, the administrator needs to troubleshoot before the window closes. The permissions available to handle that work, however, may cover more infrastructure and remain active longer than the task requires.

Traditional privileged access management can reduce risk through credential vaulting and rotation, Caulfield wrote. But he argued that operational friction can undermine those controls when teams create exceptions to complete urgent work. Those exceptions can leave credentials and privileges usable after the immediate need has passed.

Cisco's stated objective is to give infrastructure teams a consistent route to the devices they manage while providing security teams with policy enforcement, temporary permissions, and a record of activity. The company describes operational convenience as part of the design, rather than treating access governance solely as a credential-management problem.

Privileged Infrastructure Access makes access an on-demand action, according to the announcement. Cryptographic identity establishes the actor, and the resulting connection provides only the privileges needed to complete the task. Those privileges expire when the task concludes.

For customers using Cisco Identity Services Engine (ISE) and Terminal Access Controller Access-Control System Plus (TACACS+), the offering can draw on existing policy and device context to govern access to Cisco routers and switches. Cisco says customers can extend the same access model to other infrastructure, including servers, Kubernetes clusters, and cloud resources.

New research suggests that the value of AI should be measured not only by the work it eliminates, but also by the instructional insight and human connection it preserves.

Cloud Security Alliance's 2026 Top Threats report ranks identity as the leading cloud security concern, followed by two new AI-related risks.

In an active social engineering campaign, cyber attackers are impersonating IT help desks and using fake passkey setup requests to compromise employee identities and gain access to enterprise cloud data.

#6911. Your Sexual Boundaries Aren't Up for Debate, Even in BDSM

Hard limits, soft limits, and why knowing the difference matters.

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

There's a pretty big difference between being open to trying something new in bed and being actually okay with it. Maybe you're curious about being tied up but aren't ready to hand over control just yet. Or perhaps dirty talk is your thing, but certain words are an immediate turnoff. Whatever your preferences, knowing where you draw the line is important, especially when BDSM and kink enter the picture.

Despite what some sexy movies might make it seem, BDSM—which stands for bondage and discipline, Dominance and submission, and/or sadism and masochism—isn’t just a free-for-all to get freaky. Instead, hard and soft limits help establish what's completely off the table, what you might be open to exploring, and what needs to happen before anyone considers trying something new.

“There's this idea that talking about sex beforehand somehow takes away from the spontaneity or excitement. I actually think the opposite. Having these conversations is what allows people to feel safe enough to explore,” says Sarah Kelleher, LCSW, a sex and relationship psychotherapist.

After all, the appeal of kink might involve stepping outside your comfort zone, but that doesn't mean anyone gets to decide where your boundaries are except you.

Ahead, sex experts explain the difference between hard and soft limits, how to figure out yours, and how to talk about them with a partner before things get interesting.

In BDSM, limits are the boundaries that determine what you're comfortable doing during sexual play, what you're not interested in, and what might require a little more discussion. They're especially important when exploring activities involving power, control, or physical sensations, where one person's idea of a good time might be another person's absolute nightmare.

Hard limits are firm, non-negotiable boundaries. They're the things you absolutely don't want to do, regardless of the circumstances. For example, someone might be comfortable with light spanking but draw the line at anything that leaves bruises. Others might rule out humiliation, certain types of penetration, or specific language during dirty talk. Whatever the reason, a hard limit means no. Full stop.

Soft limits, on the other hand, are conditional boundaries. These are activities you might be curious about but aren't necessarily ready or willing to try without certain conditions in place. Maybe hair pulling is off-limits unless it's a gentle tug, or you're curious about bondage but only willing to explore it with loose, soft ties.

Importantly, a soft limit isn't permission to keep asking until someone changes their mind. “'Soft' doesn't mean optional, negotiable by default, or something that can be ignored,” says Lisa Finn, a sex educator at Babeland. Until someone explicitly agrees to explore an activity, that boundary still stands.

And while limits are particularly important in BDSM, they're not exclusive to kink. Everyone has sexual boundaries, whether they're experimenting with elaborate power dynamics or sticking to their favorite tried-and-true positions.

Figuring out your boundaries isn't always as simple as knowing what turns you on and what doesn't. Especially if you're new to kink, you might be curious about something in theory but have no idea how you'd feel about it in practice. Don’t stress. You don't need to have an entire BDSM bucket list figured out before you start exploring.

One place to start is with a Yes/No/Maybe list, which breaks down different sexual activities into things you're interested in, things you're definitely not interested in, and things you might consider under the right circumstances. “Your ‘maybes’ are often where your soft limits live,” says Sarah Tomchesson, a certified sex educator and director of marketing and education at Magic Wand.

But don't assume every maybe is secretly a yes waiting to happen. Kelleher recommends getting curious about why something makes you hesitant. Are you nervous because it's unfamiliar, or because you genuinely don't want to do it? There's a difference between feeling excited about a new experience and feeling like you should be excited about it.

It can also help to pay attention to how your body responds when you imagine certain activities. Tomchesson suggests noticing changes in your breathing, tension, or overall sense of comfort. You can also leave something in the maybe category indefinitely. There's no deadline for figuring out your kinks, and you're not obligated to try something just because it sounds interesting.

The best time to talk about your sexual boundaries? Before you're naked, turned on, or halfway through a scene. While discussing your limits might not sound like the sexiest form of foreplay, it's a lot easier to make informed decisions when nobody's caught up in the moment.

Start by talking about what you're interested in exploring, what's completely off-limits, and what you'd only consider under certain circumstances. Be specific, too. If you're comfortable with dirty talk but certain words are off-limits, say exactly which ones. And make sure you're asking about your partner's boundaries just as much as you're sharing your own.

Before getting started, you'll also want to establish a safeword or another clear signal that means it's time to stop. Finn recommends the traffic light system: Green means you're comfortable continuing, yellow means you need to slow down or pause and check in, and red means stop immediately. Nonverbal signals are also important for situations where speaking might be difficult or impossible.

And what if your partner's limits don't line up with yours? That doesn't mean someone needs to give in. “I actually prefer the word collaboration over compromise when we're talking about sex,” says Kelleher. Rather than trying to convince someone to participate in an activity they're uncomfortable with, look for other ways to explore the desires you share.

Finally, keep the conversation going after sex. Tomchesson recommends checking in about what felt good, what didn't, and what you'd like to do differently next time. Your boundaries aren't a one-time discussion, and neither is consent.

Absolutely. Your boundaries can change over time, but that doesn't mean they have to. Maybe a kink you once found intimidating starts to sound appealing, or something you used to love no longer does it for you. Both are completely normal, and neither requires an explanation.

“Our sexuality isn't static, and neither are our boundaries,” says Kelleher. As your experiences, relationships, and interests evolve, your limits might shift, too. Some maybes stay maybes forever, and that's perfectly fine.

If you're interested in revisiting a soft limit, have that conversation with your partner outside of sexual play. Discuss what you're curious about, what conditions would help you feel comfortable, and what you'd like to keep off-limits. Agreeing to try something once doesn't mean you've signed up for it indefinitely. Consent can be withdrawn at any time.

There's also a big difference between someone willingly exploring a boundary and a partner pressuring them to change it. “A soft limit isn't a boundary that's meant to be pushed,” says Kelleher. Repeatedly asking, bargaining, or making someone feel guilty for saying no isn't healthy sexual exploration.

If a partner crosses an established limit, stop the activity immediately. Sometimes boundaries are crossed through genuine misunderstandings, but intentionally ignoring a limit or safeword is a consent violation. You get to decide what happens next, whether that's having a conversation, taking space, or ending the relationship.

Your limits belong to you, and you don't owe anyone a more adventurous version of yourself. The whole point of exploring your sexuality is figuring out what feels good to you, not convincing yourself to enjoy something for someone else's benefit.

Companies are running into problems as they give artificial intelligence more control over critical business applications, with 84% reporting at least one AI-related incident in the past year, according to a new survey from identity security firm Delinea.

The company's 2026 Application Access Governance report found that some organizations have experienced incorrect financial transactions and AI actions they couldn't trace or explain. The findings raise concerns about whether enterprises have enough oversight of AI systems that can make decisions and take actions on their own

The findings are based on an independent survey of 1,000 senior finance and finance technology leaders in the United States and United Kingdom. The AI-specific analysis included 879 respondents whose organizations use the technology in critical business applications.

The report found that 35% of surveyed organizations have deployed AI agents capable of taking actions within business applications. However, only 11% of organizations using AI said their governance and security controls were keeping pace with deployment.

Delinea also found gaps in centralized visibility into agent deployment and permissions, including who owns agents and who approved their use.

Some respondents reported segregation-of-duties conflicts involving AI, while others identified agents that continued operating after the employees who deployed them had left the organization.

The research also highlighted gaps in monitoring access across business systems. Only 46% of respondents said they continuously monitor and manage cumulative access risks across applications.

Those gaps can make it difficult to identify segregation-of-duties conflicts, in which a person or agent has permissions across systems that should be kept separate.

Despite those issues, 95% of respondents said they were confident their access controls would withstand scrutiny in an external audit.

However, 26% reported access-related audit findings that had recurred in consecutive years, while 25% said some findings had remained unresolved for three or more years.

Only 22% of organizations had a single named owner for each application, with ownership tracked in one system of record.

Meanwhile, 60% reported an increase over the past two years in the number of critical applications operating outside the scope of access governance or Sarbanes-Oxley Act compliance.

The findings highlight the oversight challenges associated with giving AI agents authority to act within business systems, particularly when organizations cannot consistently identify who owns an agent or trace its actions.

Frank Vukovits, chief security scientist at Delinea, said organizations remain responsible for the actions AI agents take within their systems.

"When an agent can approve a transaction or change permissions, the business owns the outcome. You need to know what every agent can do, who owns it, and who approved it, before an auditor asks or your financials are misstated," Vukovits said.

For the full report, go to the Delinea site.

In an Aug. 20 Dear Colleague Letter from Kirsten Baesler, assistant secretary of the United States Department of Education's Office of Elementary and Secondary Education, ED outlines new guidance to frame the national conversation around the educational value of technology in schools.

New research suggests that the value of AI should be measured not only by the work it eliminates, but also by the instructional insight and human connection it preserves.

Cloud Security Alliance's 2026 Top Threats report ranks identity as the leading cloud security concern, followed by two new AI-related risks.

In an active social engineering campaign, cyber attackers are impersonating IT help desks and using fake passkey setup requests to compromise employee identities and gain access to enterprise cloud data.

#6906. How to Master the Indie Sleaze Makeup Revival

The antithesis of clean girl beauty is back, this time with a little more intention and polish.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

There’s a point when a smoky eye goes from elegant with a slight edge to worn in and a bit reckless...in a good way. As the antithesis of the clean girl aesthetic and all its minimalist, nude, no-color chicness, the indie sleaze, grungy makeup look of the ’90s and early 2000s has returned, but with a few updates. Makeup artist and founder of KJH.brand, Katie Jane Hughes, describes how this 2.0 take differs from its first iterations, why it’s making a comeback, and exactly what you need to create your version of the gritty, slightly imperfect look.

Clean, barely there beauty has dominated runways and social feeds in the past few years, but as we reach peak maxxing in other areas, it looks like makeup is finally following suit. “I think people are craving fun again. And they’ve begun seeing it on the likes of Gabbriette, in recent Gucci fashion shows, or in makeup done by Sam Visser and other makeup artists like Yasmin Istanbouli,” Hughes tells ELLE when asked why the look is seeing a revival right now.

Grunge, in both beauty and fashion, has always leaned a little dark and dirty, but Hughes says the look is now paired with more elegant and chic details. “[It’s] more purposeful and less disheveled outfits paired with it,” she notes. The fully loaded emo energy is being replaced by bursts of color like the hot pink outline seen on Alex Consani during Gucci’s SS27 The Store Show—under the guidance of Sam Visser.

The slept-in grittiness of grunge makeup looks more involved to create intentionally, but you actually only need a few products. Hughes suggests starting with a good black eye crayon like the KJH.brand Precision Sculpture Stylo in Forge. “It’s helpful to have an eyeliner that’s not too pointed and precise,” she advises. Jet-black eyeshadow: “I really love the Plume eyeshadows from VOILETTE_FR in black shade. It really lets you stretch color big and wide and offers the blendability of a cream with the buildability of a powder,” she adds. She may also top the lids in her version with something greasy like the KJH.brand Hyper Shine Serum.

If you’re trying the look for the first time, Hughes says to keep it simple. “Start with one product, like the Stylo. Smudge around the lash line, blend, and you’re done,” Hughes instructs. As for dos and don’ts, Hughes says, do put some concealer on a small brush, prime it in, and clean up any mistakes. Don’t pair indie sleaze with a bold lip. “Keeping the rest of the makeup nondescript or barely there makes it look cooler,” Hughes notes.

#6905. Report: Cyber Criminals Move Toward More Targeted Phishing Attacks

Cyber criminals are moving away from mass phishing scams and focusing on more targeted attacks, increasingly using trusted websites and services to trick victims, according to a new report from KnowBe4. The security firm found that spear-phishing attacks increased 41% over the past year.

The company's Phishing Threat Trends Report, Vol. 8 found that overall phishing volume increased 29.8% year over year. Organizations received an average of one dangerous e-mail for every 733 messages, but the bigger concern is how attackers are changing their approach.

Rather than sending the same fraudulent message to thousands of people, criminals are spending more time researching their targets and creating convincing messages that appear to come from trusted coworkers, businesses, and technology platforms.

The findings are based on threat data from KnowBe4 Defend, the company's inbound e-mail security platform, which processes approximately 1.15 billion event signals daily.

KnowBe4 found that several traditional phishing methods declined significantly compared with 2025. Mobile-focused scams dropped 47.3%, advance-fee fraud fell 31.3%, and missed-message scams declined 11.3%.

Meanwhile, spear phishing increased 41.1%, pre-attack reconnaissance rose 33.7%, and company impersonation climbed 28%.

The shift suggests that attackers are finding more value in carefully crafted scams that exploit existing business relationships and workplace routines.

Business e-mail compromise (BEC) remains particularly common, accounting for 51.2% of phishing e-mails analyzed in the report. Human resources, finance departments, and executive leadership are among the most frequently targeted groups.

Attackers also appear to be timing their campaigns around normal business activity. KnowBe4 found that 90.4% of phishing messages arrive Monday through Friday, with Monday accounting for the largest share at 20.6%.

One of the report's more troubling findings involves the growing use of legitimate technology platforms to deliver malicious content.

KnowBe4 found that phishing attacks were twice as likely to originate from legitimate infrastructure in the third quarter of 2026 compared with the previous quarter.

That includes AI services, where attackers are taking advantage of trusted domains to host fake developer consoles, login prompts, and credential-stealing pages. The report also identified a 228% increase in AI-brand impersonation attacks between March and August.

Because these malicious pages can operate on legitimate domains with valid security certificates, users may not see the usual warning signs associated with suspicious websites.

"For years, security advice has focused on a predictable checklist: Check the sender address, hover over the link, and look for the padlock," said Jack Chapman, senior vice president of threat intelligence at KnowBe4.

"While still foundational, that guidance is becoming less useful by the day."

The findings echo a recent report about management software and AI e-mail assistants. Research from Fortra and Barracuda showed how criminals are using legitimate administrative tools and hidden AI instructions to manipulate users and compromise systems.

The heads of OpenAI, Anthropic, and Hugging Face recently joined AI researcher Yoshua Bengio at the United Nations headquarters in New York to warn the UN Security Council about the security risks of increasingly capable AI.

#6900. Study: Sign-In Problems Are Primary Drivers of Help Desk Tickets

Sign-in problems account for up to 53% of help desk tickets at individual enterprises, but network issues can take significantly longer to resolve, according to a new study from ControlUp.

Analyzing 1,069,110 help desk tickets, the IT operations company identified password resets, account lockouts, and multifactor authentication (MFA) issues as major drivers of support demand.

At one large enterprise, account-related tickets took a median of 11 minutes to resolve, compared with 50 hours for network problems. Meanwhile, 87% of network and phone tickets were reassigned between teams, compared with 20% of account tickets.

MFA enrollment and re-enrollment problems appeared at 40% of organizations, with one in five linking these issues to employees replacing or upgrading their phones. Virtual desktop problems appeared at 55% of organizations analyzed.

Traditional phone support remained a major channel for IT assistance.

Among organizations that recorded how support requests were submitted, 57% reported that phone calls accounted for approximately two-thirds or more of contacts. At one organization, that figure reached 99%.

Machine-generated tickets from monitoring, security, backup, and patching systems appeared at half of the organizations studied. At 15%, automated tickets represented between 25% and 50% of all requests.

The research covered enterprises across retail, healthcare, financial services, manufacturing, construction, energy, and IT services.

ControlUp said the findings highlight the need to evaluate recurring problems and their operational impact rather than relying solely on ticket volume and resolution speed.

"IT organizations have traditionally measured support performance by how quickly they close tickets, but that doesn't tell the whole story," said Simon Townsend, senior vice president of marketing and office of the CTO at ControlUp.

"A password reset that takes a few minutes and an infrastructure failure that disrupts an entire location shouldn't carry the same operational weight."

For more information, go to the ControlUp site.

#6898. Trinity Rodman and Ben Shelton’s Complete Relationship Timeline

A love born on TikTok, their relationship quickly captured the internet’s attention.

Being a professional athlete takes an inordinate amount of hard work and dedication, which is why Trinity Rodman’s romance with Ben Shelton makes so much sense. While Rodman is a soccer star, playing for the Washington Spirit and the United States women’s national soccer team, Shelton is a professional tennis champ, representing the U.S. at tournaments across the globe, including Wimbledon. Since making their relationship public in spring 2025, interest in Shelton and Rodman’s connection has only intensified.

In November 2026, multiple outlets, including People, reported that the pair had broken up. Here’s what you need to know about Rodman and Shelton’s relationship timeline.

Rodman is prolific on TikTok, with more than 305,000 followers as of August 2025. As a result, it makes perfect sense that the star athlete appeared to tease her new romance with Shelton on the platform. The pair hinted that they were both “shooting their shot” with one another in a TikTok trend in March 2025, via espnW’s Instagram account. And it wasn’t long before the two athletes decided to hard-launch their romance on social media.

On March 18, Shelton shared a series of photos on Instagram, the last of which was a mirror selfie taken with Rodman in an elevator. “I been focused, but I’m steady climbin’, I’m nowhere near my peak,” he captioned the post. The soccer player commented on her boyfriend’s post, writing, “Shooters shoot I guess,” suggesting that the TikTok most certainly paid off, as reported by The Mirror US.

After Shelton and Rodman revealed their relationship on Instagram, tennis pro Frances Tiafoe suggested he was actually responsible for introducing the pair. During an appearance on the TikTok account Overtime Tennis on March 18, 2025, Tiafoe said, “By the way, I don’t think people realize, I was like, the one who [connected them]—if we’re going to be real.”

“I’m happy for him, man,” Tiafoe said of Shelton. “The elevator photo out the gate...everybody knows who Trinity Rodman is. [The photo] at the end of the carousel, sick.”

However, Shelton and Rodman later denied Tiafoe’s version of events, suggesting he wasn’t responsible for them meeting. “I don’t know if I want everyone to know the story [of how we met], but I can assure you Frances was not the reason we met or got together. I don’t know why he’s taking credit for that,” Rodman told ESPN’s SportsCenter. “Me and Ben can both cut that out. It was solely a me and Ben thing.”

Shelton also shot down Tiafoe’s claims, telling the New York Post, “We’d like talked about her once, twice before and I was like, ‘But come on, man. You didn’t do anything.’ Putting us together, like that’s a bit of a stretch.” He continued, “[Rodman] was kind of annoyed by that. She was like, ‘Come on, bro.’ I think she said something to him a couple times. But we’re all friends.”

After Shelton took their relationship public with his March 18, 2025 Instagram post, Rodman returned the favor by posting an adorable photo on social media, too. The picture showed Rodman gazing up at her tennis pro boyfriend, whose face was hidden by a baseball cap. Tagging Shelton in the photo, Rodman captioned her March 27 Instagram post, “Dibs.” Shelton commented on the post, writing, “Whoever that is he won [for sure].”

From that point on, the romance between the two athletes seems to have only grown stronger. For instance, in an Instagram carousel posted on April 5, 2025, Shelton shared a short clip as he walked alongside Rodman, captioning the post, “In n out of time zones,” suggesting they were traveling together.

The public’s excitement about the romance wasn’t lost on Rodman and Shelton. During an appearance on ESPN’s SportsCenter in March 2025, Rodman spoke about her decision to go public with the relationship, saying, “I mean, I’m just another person in a relationship and people know about it.” She continued, “I’m obviously very happy.”

In his April 2025 interview with the New York Post, Shelton discussed his romance with Rodman, and the incredible response they had received after revealing their relationship on Instagram. “It’s been great, obviously,” the 22-year-old athlete said. “I guess I didn’t think it would blow up as much as it did. That’s not why I did it.”

The tennis pro also suggested that “posting your girlfriend on Instagram is a more normal thing,” so he didn’t necessarily think the photos would make such an impact. “I realized, ‘Wow, people care about my life that much,’” he told the outlet. “But yeah, [we’re] happy for sure.”

The same month, Rodman shared a TikTok video of herself hugging her boyfriend, with the caption, “My safety blanket...you make everyday better.”

In April 2025, celebrated Olympian Rodman traveled to Munich in support of Shelton, who made it all the way to the men’s singles final in the BMW Open 2025. Rodman made her debut in the player’s box when Shelton beat Francisco Cerundolo in the men’s singles semifinal, and was spotted talking to her boyfriend’s dad, Bryan Shelton, during the competition. “She’s a good luck charm,” @tennischannel wrote on Instagram. Although Shelton ultimately lost the final to Germany’s Alexander Zverev, Rodman’s presence at the tournament made their relationship status quite clear.

In July 2025, Shelton made it to the quarterfinals at Wimbledon, where he ultimately lost to Italian tennis player Jannik Sinner. Rodman supported her boyfriend at the tournament in London, and she also criticized commentators for calling her the wrong name and mentioning her estranged dad, former NBA player Dennis Rodman.

“For those who don’t know...my name is TRINITY not Tiffany,” Rodman wrote on her Instagram Stories, via Sports Illustrated. “Also, for Ben’s matches he has his family there as his support system, which includes his dad... my dads not even in MY life no need to bring him up during HIS matches when I don’t even want him talked about during mine.”

As well as competing across Europe throughout the summer, Shelton still found time to savor some vacation days with his girlfriend. On May 20, Shelton shared two sweet photos on Instagram, which he captioned, “Happy birthday beautiful girl...I love you.” The Olympic gold medalist responded in the comments, writing, “I luhhh you.”

The following month, Rodman shared footage showing her holding hands with her boyfriend as they walked around Europe. “No one else,” she captioned the Instagram post. On July 5, the Washington Spirit player shared another cute photo with Shelton, with the caption, “Watching you with googly eyes,” to which he responded, “Why am I cheesin so big.”

As both Shelton and Rodman are accustomed to traveling the world for their careers, they’ve had to adjust to dating while being in completely different places. “It has its challenges, but it’s definitely worth it,” Shelton told Town & Country. “It’s not something that we’re not used to—it’s been the story of our lives, especially me being on the road all the time. It’s just normal life stuff.”

In August 2025, Rodman made her long-awaited return to soccer after taking time off to deal with a back injury. Prior to returning to work, Rodman shared vacation snaps with Shelton, along with training videos, writing on Instagram, “Not the summer that I had in mind but still loved every moment...can’t wait to be back on the field.”

On August 3, Rodman’s team, the Washington Spirit, played against the Portland Thorns in the National Women’s Soccer League. After joining the game for the final 14 minutes, Rodman was able to score the winning goal, cementing her triumphant return to the sport.

Shelton wasn’t able to support his girlfriend in person, as he was in Toronto for the 2025 National Bank Open. However, he celebrated her win from afar, telling ATP Tour, “One year anniversary from her incredible goal at the Olympics, hadn’t played in 100-something days and she goes in and in 15 minutes scores a banger. It’s pretty cool, especially [because] she was emotional. I’m always happy for her.”

On June 14, Rodman shared a selfie with Shelton on Instagram, writing simply, “#1 fan I love you ❤️.”

Rodman traveled to Montreal to surprise Shelton during the Canadian Open after playing with the Washington Spirit in Washington, D.C., on August 8. She documented his reaction on her Instagram Stories, writing, “Surprised his ass, happy matchday in Montreal 😘❤️ @benshelton.”

Shelton went on to win the tournament, and Rodman celebrated his victory in an August 14 Instagram post. Alongside a photo of the couple walking together on the tennis court, she wrote, “Drive, dedication, and commitment. Back to back Canada champ! Celebrate every win baby ❤️ I love you.”

During an August 2026 appearance on Kylie Kelce’s Not Gonna Lie podcast, Rodman opened up about how her relationship with Shelton began, revealing that she was the one who initially reached out.

“Everything I do is just embarrassing. I’m really embarrassed,” she said.

Rodman explained just how quickly she wanted to get to know Shelton: “I fully committed to this guy that I didn’t even meet yet, for him to stay at my house for the weekend. It’s actually crazy, it’s insane, but it worked out.”

She also discussed why she personally embraces being called a WAG despite being a professional athlete herself.

“For me, I think I like the term personally because, like I said before, I feel like 24/7 I’m on in front of cameras…and so I think I use the term WAG for me to simplify what I’m doing,” she said. “For me, saying WAG, it’s me being like, ‘I’m Ben’s girlfriend.’ But I can understand why [the term] is so hated.”

Rodman added that having a partner who understands the demands of professional sports has been particularly meaningful to her.

“It’s the best feeling in the world, honestly. I think to know we’re both on the grind, running our bodies to the ground, always exhausted, doing what we love, and then to also be able to be a little like a WAG in a sense, it feels so good for us,” she said.

Rodman showed her support for Shelton after he beat Carlos Alcaraz in the quarterfinal of the US Open. The competitive match ended at 3:30 A.M. “My 🌎,” she wrote on her Instagram Story.

She was also in the stands cheering him on:

On November 24, People reported that the couple had broken up. Days before, Shelton responded “no” when asked if he would attend Rodman and the Washington Spirit’s championship game against the Kansas City Current on November 21.

#6896. Enterprise AI Spending Outpaces Adoption Growth, Report Finds

Enterprise AI spending is growing faster than the number of organizations paying for it, according to a new Menlo Ventures report that points to a shift from buying access for employees to financing work performed by agents.

The venture capital firm's 2026 State of Enterprise AI report estimates deployment spending reached a $176.8 billion annualized run rate by September, up from $33 billion in 2025. The share of enterprises paying for AI rose from 55% to 65%.

A larger bill can reflect broader use inside an organization, but it does not independently establish that the additional work is useful or profitable.

Menlo's methodology describes revenue figures as annualized run rates. The $176.8 billion estimate therefore should not be read as $176.8 billion already spent during the first nine months of 2026.

The report also distinguishes unusually heavy spenders from the typical respondent. Average reported spending reached 4.7 times the prior year's level, while the median reached 2.2 times. That gap suggests a smaller group of large spenders drove the average substantially.

The practical implication is that an industry growth figure may be a poor benchmark for an individual organization's budget. Buyers need to understand their own workload growth and the results it produces.

"Agents are changing the economics of software," Menlo principal Derek Xiao said in the firm's announcement.

Menlo reports that agents account for 80% of tokens on OpenRouter, a service providing access to multiple models. That measures activity on one platform, rather than all enterprise AI use. Token consumption also measures processing volume, rather than completed business outcomes.

An agent that makes repeated attempts can consume more resources without delivering proportionately more value. For procurement teams, the relevant comparison is consequently the cost of completing acceptable work, including the review it requires.

Menlo estimates that Anthropic, OpenAI, and Google capture 69% of enterprise AI deployment spending. That measure is distinct from their shares of enterprise language-model API spending, a narrower category. The figures should not be used interchangeably.

The report draws on a Morning Consult survey of nearly 400 U.S. enterprise decision-makers responsible for more than $4 billion in AI spending. Market totals are modeled estimates informed by surveys, public filings, and third-party reports. Menlo discloses investments in Anthropic and OpenRouter.

A separate announcement from Information Services Group describes buyers putting more emphasis on the economics and reliability of production deployments. ISG says ongoing security and observability work remains necessary as organizations scale.

That provides a useful distinction between market demand and business value. Suppliers can generate growing revenue while customers still determine which deployments justify the cost.

For enterprise leaders, the new spending estimate strengthens the case for examining usage beyond license counts. It leaves the harder purchasing question with the customer: How much worthwhile work did the increased spending actually buy?

Menlo's State of Enterprise AI report is available here.

#6895. Phishing Threats Totaled 7.3 Billion in Q3 2026

E-mail phishing threats reached 7.3 billion in Q3 2026, with September activity increasing 41% from August, according to Microsoft Threat Intelligence.

According to a report from Microsoft Threat Intelligence and the Microsoft Defender Security Research Team, phishing detections reached nearly 3 billion in September alone.

The findings also highlighted changes in attacker techniques, including business-service impersonation, credential theft, and social engineering through Microsoft Teams.

The Redmond-based firm recognized approximately 129 million malicious messages spoofing the sender domains of tracked business services during Q3.

DocuSign was the most frequently spoofed brand in September, accounting for 29% of the month's tracked spoofing activity. Adobe, SurveyMonkey, and Barracuda also experienced increases toward the end of the quarter.

The research found that some malicious messages were delivered despite failing e-mail authentication checks and being identified as threats.

In September, tenant- or user-level security overrides applied to 63% of these messages with a recorded security outcome — most involved tenant-level exceptions.

Microsoft warned that overly broad exceptions intended to support legitimate business communications can weaken protection against impersonation attacks.

The company emphasized that these findings do not establish whether recipients opened the messages or whether accounts were compromised.

Microsoft also recorded approximately 141,000 malicious Teams call attempts during Q3.

Attackers increasingly used ordinary personal names instead of explicitly impersonating IT support personnel. Such display names accounted for 46% of an observed sample in July, rising to 88% in September.

Meanwhile, QR code phishing declined 67% compared to Q2, totaling roughly 12.1 million attacks.

CAPTCHA-gated phishing activity increased toward the end of the quarter, reaching 3.1 million detected attacks in September. SVG attachments played a significant role in the increase.

Credential theft remained the primary objective of malicious attachment campaigns, with approximately 94% to 96% of payload-based attacks directing users to phishing sites or fraudulent sign-in pages.

Microsoft recommended reviewing Exchange Online Protection and Defender for Office 365 configurations, enabling Safe Links and Safe Attachments, and strengthening authentication protections.

The company also advised organizations to review security exceptions and verify the identities of external Teams contacts before responding to suspicious requests.

For more information, go to the Microsoft blog post.

#6891. Inside a Denver Tudor That Blends English Eccentricity With Family-Friendly Design

Refurbished original details give the renovation a touch of old-soul charm.

When designer Kaitlyn Hoffman and her family returned to the United States after eight years in London, they had grown accustomed to the tighter quarters that city living often dictates. Needless to say, settling into a five-bedroom Tudor in Denver’s Cheesman Park neighborhood was a big leap. Built in 1900, the house was in good condition when Hoffman and her husband bought it, but earlier renovations had left it feeling at odds with its architectural style.

“The biggest design challenge was anticipating how we would actually live in the home,” Hoffman says. Because they renovated the space before moving in, she had to guess at everything from where they’d gather to how the house would work for their two young kids. “Ultimately, it was an exercise in designing for a life we hadn’t quite experienced yet and trusting our instincts about how we wanted to live.”

The nine-month project, which also launched her firm, Studio Proper Design, restored the home’s Tudor character. Hoffman and her partners at Alter Design + Build gutted the primary and powder bathrooms, overhauled the kitchen, and made smaller updates almost everywhere else. Along the way, plenty of the home’s charming original details got a second life: A pair of French doors found in the basement now anchors a custom pantry, and diamond-pattern mullions in the primary bath windows make the room look as though it had always been part of the house.

The result is a home that reflects Hoffman’s signature “Brit-ish” style: layered, collected, and not too precious. “I think the magic is in the mix,” she says, crediting her years abroad with inspiring a fondness for eccentricity, coziness, and humor in her design projects. Even the house’s lore fits the mood. Neighbors insist it’s the former home of ‘60s country-rock group Nitty Gritty Dirt Band—though Hoffman admits she’s never been able to confirm it. “Whether it’s true or not, it’s fun to imagine that these walls might have witnessed a little rock-and-roll history.”

FAST FACTSDesigner: Kaitlyn Hoffman of Studio Proper DesignDesign Partner: Alter Design + BuildStylist: Natalie WaradyLocation: Denver, ColoradoThe Space: A five-bedroom, three-and-a-half-bathroom Tudor across 6,345 square feet

A place where the whole family gathers and hosts, this living room is packed with personality and beloved furnishings, many of which have changed hands in the family throughout the years. While the decor is decidedly eclectic, the architecture itself is classic and stately. “We wanted this room to have the elegance of a traditional English drawing room, so we introduced egg-and-dart crown molding and a hand-carved Louis XV–style marble fireplace to give the space a greater sense of formality and architectural presence,” Hoffman shares.

According to Hoffman, this area saw a true “everything but the kitchen cabinets” renovation. Existing wall ovens gave way to a freestanding range with a custom hood, and a new pantry took their place. It’s built around a pair of original French doors she found in the basement, adding key storage while hiding away the appliances.

To soften the modern lines, she added a paneled backsplash, unlacquered brass hardware, and sealed walnut butcher block countertops for a warm, rustic touch. “I wanted a kitchen that worked hard for our family but felt every bit as inviting as the rest of the house,” Hoffman adds.

Though the dining room shares space with the living room, both areas needed to have distinct identities. To make guests feel like they’re entering into a completely separate room, Hoffman sourced several statement-making pieces. “I found all six antique Louis-style dining chairs on Facebook Marketplace for just $100, while the mirrored faux-bamboo armoire is a favorite piece we brought back with us from London,” Hoffman says.

Tucked beneath the stairs between the kitchen and living room is a clever nook for mixing up the drink of the hour. “This multipurpose space houses my husband’s (many!) coffee supplies by day and transforms into a bar by night,” Hoffman says. Like in the kitchen, she preserved the existing cabinetry and gave it a fresh coat of blue paint, new Negresco honed granite countertops, wallpaper, and updated fixtures. Now, it rivals the kitchen as the go-to entertaining spot in the home.

While gut-renovating this powder room, Hoffman fell in love with the botanical wallpaper, and she would’ve covered the entire space in it if it were a little more kid-friendly. “I opted for wainscoting on the lower half of the walls to protect the wallpaper from sticky fingers,” she says.

This is one of two generously sized guest bedroom suites, so friends and family will never feel short-changed when it’s time to hit the hay. It also has some of the most intriguing decorative details in the entire house. “The gingham four-poster bed is the star of the room, and I love the playful contrast between its bold check and the floral Roman shades,” she says. The wall accents are equally as enviable. “The framed Hermès scarf is another longtime favorite, which I sourced at Round Top over a decade ago, before Round Top was cool!”

Hoffman took a light-touch approach to her daughter’s room, knowing that the seven-year-old would eventually want to put her own stylistic signature on it. “I wanted to create a room that felt charming and playful without being overly childish, with pieces she could continue to enjoy as she grows,” she notes. Beyond painting the walls a pretty blush pink, the only real change here was adding custom Roman shades, which give the room a more tailored, finished feel.

This primary bathroom saw a full gut renovation to bring it up to the standards of modern living. Along with adding his-and-hers sinks and a freestanding tub, Hoffman took the opportunity to correct an architectural eyesore: the two mismatched windows that created irritating asymmetry. “We ultimately replaced both windows, incorporating diamond-pattern mullions in the upper third to complement the home’s Tudor architecture,” she adds. “It was a significant expense, but it gave us greater flexibility with the layout and brought a sense of continuity to the room.”

This guest room is an homage to the family’s life in London, as much of the decor came from their former home across the pond. The art, too, was sourced from a local printmaker whose studio was down the street in the family’s old neighborhood. Any new additions here were smaller and strategic. “The Schumacher floral fabric on the curtains and pillows became the thread that tied everything together, bringing color, pattern, and a sense of cohesion to the room,” Hoffman says.

The kids’ bathroom was reworked with total flexibility in mind. “With a son and a daughter sharing the space, I wanted a palette that felt playful and fresh without leaning either too feminine or masculine,” Hoffman notes. While the existing floor tile stayed, she added a custom double vanity so both kids can brush their teeth at the same time, as well as painted beadboard wainscoting to extend the country-home atmosphere into the washroom.

Kaitlyn Hoffman is the founder of Studio Proper, a boutique residential interior design firm located in Denver, Colorado. She’s a British citizen and lifelong Anglophile whose signature style was honed over nearly a decade living in London. Her design sensibility takes inspiration from traditional English interiors but adds a modern, practical twist.
